08D5|  5   ;***** Comparisons of complex things
5|  5  "
08D5|  5   ; Beware that many comparisons work only because compiler restricts you
08D5|  5   ;   to = and <> on certain types.
08D5|  5   ; The opcode tells what relation is being tested
08D5|  5   ;   the next byte indicates the type of the things being compared
08D5|  5   ;   if arrays are being compared, the next GBDE is the array size
08D5|  5   ; Tests allowed...
08D5|  5   ; Boolean:  all relations.  stuff is on the stack.
08D5|  5   ; Real:             all relations.  stuff is on the stack.
08D5|  5   ; Set:      =, <>, <= (subset), >= (superset).  stuff is on the stack.
08D5|  5   ; String:   all relations. pointers to stuff are on stack.
08D5|  5   ; Arrays and records:   =, <>. pointers to stuff on stack
08D5|  5   ;
08D5|  5   ;  after CSETUP flags are result of a-b.
08D5|  5   
08D5| CD ****  -   CEQU    CALL    CSETUP
08D8| CA ****  -  (JP Z,PSHTRU1
08DB| 21 0000  -   PSHFLS1 LD  HL,0000H
08DE| E5  2  (PUSH    HL
08DF| C3 A403  -  (JP BACK1
08E2|  5   
  -   CNEQ    CALL    CSETUP
08E5| CA DB08  -  (JP  Z,PSHFLS1
08D9* E808
PAGE -  30  INTERP    FILE: ARITH.TEXT   


08E8| 21 0100  -   PSHTRU1 LD  HL,0001H
08EB| E5  2  (PUSH    HL
08EC| C3 A403  -  (JP  BACK1
08EF|  5   
08EF| CD ****  -   CGTR    CALL    CSETUP
08F2| DA DB08  -  (JP      C,PSHFLS1
08F5| C2 E808  -  (JP      NZ,PSHTRU1
08F8| C3 DB08  -  (JP      PSHFLS1
08FB|  5   
08FB| CD ****  -   CLEQ    CALL    CSETUP
08FE| DA E808  -  (JP      C,PSHTRU1
0901| C2 DB08  -  (JP      NZ,PSHFLS1
0904| C3 E808  -  (JP      PSHTRU1

0B82|  5   ; The algorithm used to build the set...
0B82|  5   ;   xx := bitter[j MOD 16];  t := j DIV 16;
0B82|  5   ;   WHILE t>i DIV 16 DO
0B82|  5   ;     BEGIN  push(xx);  xx := <all ones>;  t := t-1  END

0B82|  5   ;   WHILE t >= 0 DO

0B82|  5   ;   push(j DIV 16 +1)    (* set size *)

0B82|  5   
0B82|  5   ; In the following,
0B82|  5   ;   idiv = i DIV 16, imod = 2*(i MOD 16),

0CB9|  5 ; Beginning of file FPL
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   ; Floating point stuff...including basic stuff like the four math
0CB9|  5   ;   functions, fix, and float; and much more esoteric stuff, like
0CB9|  5   ;   transcendental functions.  All routines of any general interest
0CB9|  5   ;   are callable.
0CB9|  5   ; Hopefully in the near future there will be an arithmetic vector table,
ing assembly procedures to your system will
0CB9|  5   ;   be able to make use of all this wonderful software.
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   ; Naming conventions used throughout the floating point package...
0CB9|  5   ;   FPCa        Floating point constant a.
0CB9|  5   ;   FPMa        Floating point macro a.  Some of these macros leave
0CB9|  5   ;               well-specified stuff in registers.
0CB9|  5   ;   FPFa        Floating point function a.  Takes argument(s) on tos,
0CB9|  5   ;               leaves result on tos, and also in EDCB (except for
0CB9|  5   ;               FPFFIX, FPFDOUB, FPFHALV).
0CB9|  5   ;   FPLa        Floating point low level function.  Not necessarily
0CB9|  5   ;               directly callable, and probably not of interest to
0CB9|  5   ;               the user.
0CB9|  5   ;   FPGa        Floating point global variable a.
0CB9|  5   ;   aLb         Floating point local variable b for function a.
tion a.  Returns
0CB9|  5   ;               Z = false, NZ = true (not yet meaningful).
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   
0CB9|  5   ;***************** BASIC FLOATING POINT ARITHMETIC *************;
0CB9|  5 ;       Based on an 8080 floating point package by John Lamping
0CB9|  5 
0CB9|  5 
0CB9|  5 ; Numbers are four byte quantities represented as...
0CB9|  5 ; [exp] [sabc v] [w x] [y z]
0CB9|  5 
0CB9|  5 ; s, a, b, c are bits, v, w, x, y, z are hex digits.
0CB9|  5 
0CB9|  5 ; Exponent is biased by 128.  Mantissa is always normalized, and includes
0CB9|  5 ;   "invisible" bit just in front of a.
0CB9|  5 
0CB9|  5 ; If exp = 0, number value is zero.
0CB9|  5

1766|  5   ; idsearch(VAR symcursor: cursrange; symbufp: ^symbufarray)
1766|  5   
1766|  5   ; The following declaration order for the compiler is assumed, as IDSCH is
1766|  5   ;  passed only ^symcursor.
array *);
1766|  5   ;    sy: symbol (* symbol = (ident..othersy), set by info in reswrdtable *);
1766|  5   ;    op: operator (* more info from reswrdtable *);
1766|  5   ;    id: alfa (* packed array [1..8] of char, gets filled with first 8 chars
1766|  5   ;          of token isolated by IDSRCH if token is an identifier *);
1766|  5   
1766|  5   ; Isolate token, converting to upper case.
1766|  5   ; If token in reswrdtable set sy and op from table,
1766|  5   ; else set st := ident, and put first 8 chars (left-justified) of

1A8D|  5   ; treesearch(rootp: ^node; VAR foundp:^node; VAR target: alfa): integer
1A8D|  5   
1A8D|  5   ;  TYPE node = RECORD
1A8D|  5   ;                key: alfa;
1A8D|  5   ;                rlink: ^node;
1A8D|  5   ;                llink: ^node
1A8D|  5   ;              END;
1A8D|  5   ;  function returns...
1A8D|  5   ;     0: foundp points to matching node
1A8D|  5   ;    +1: foundp points to a leaf, and target>foundp.key
1A8D|  5   ;    -1: foundp points to a leaf, and target<foundp.key

1E6D|  5   ;    The special TEK bootstrap, which is for files that include both
1E6D|  5   ;       the interpreter and the P-code program to execute.
1E6D|  5   
1E6D|  5           .IF     TEK
1E6D|  5           .ENDC
1E6D|  5   
1E6D|  5   ;    The complex bootstrap.  So termed not because it is complex, but
1E6D|  5   ;       because the primary booter that read in the interpreter also
1E6D|  5   ;       initialized the P-machine, in a like manner to the booter below
1E6D|  5   
1E6D| E1  2   CBOOT   POP     HL              ; get UCSD bios location...
1E6E| 22 0302  -  (LD      (BIOSJP),HL     ; ...save it in a fixed place...
1E71| C3 A403  -  (JP      BACK1           ; ...and start interpreting
1E74|  5   
1E74|  5   
rimary booters that simply read in the interp
1E74|  5   ; This is a Pascal-system loader, it assumes that
1E74|  5   ;the complete interpreter and booter have been
1E74|  5   ;loaded by the host machine. It assumes that on
PAGE - 113  INTERP    FILE: BOOT.TEXT   


1E74|  5   ;unit 4 ,block 2 is a directory with the pascal
1E74|  5   ;operating system 'SYSTEM.PASCAL'. The booter
1E74|  5   ;reads this, initializes the interpreter to enter
1E74|  5   ;the system outer block and goes to it.
1E74|  5   
1E74|  5   ;     Six easy steps toward the realization of Pascal.
1E74|  5   ;  1: initialize all I/O drivers, size memory
1E74|  5   ;  2: read directory, find 'SYSTEM.PASCAL'
1E74|  5   ;  3: read block zero and set up SEGTBL
1E74|  5   ;  4: read in segment zero
1E74|  5   ;  5: set up machine state for seg 0 proc 1
1E74|  5   ;  6: GO FOR IT.
1E74|  5   
1E74|  5  (; These declarations are for Step 2.
1E74| 0D  2   SYSTLE  .BYTE   0DH             ;length byte of String
5 4D 2E   (.ASCII  "SYSTEM.PASCAL" ;characters of String
1E7C| 50 41 53 43 41 4C

1E82| 0000  0   DENTP   .WORD   0               ;gets set by this Step
1E84|  5

1E86| 00  2   SEGCNT  .BYTE   0
